And, um, even at my family, which is... that's not me. That's not how I've ever handled things. I usually keep it together. I just... I don't want you to think I'm a bad guy or anything. I just need a little help getting back to normal, if that makes sense?\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling down, hopeless, or have you lost interest in things you usually enjoy, like hobbies or time with family?\n\nPatient: No, no, I wouldn't say hopeless. I mean, I have a lot to be thankful for, right? I definitely don't want to sound ungrateful. But, um... I guess... well, this is a little hard to admit. \n\nLike, my wife will make a nice dinner, or my kids will be talking about soccer just like normal, and... I know I *should* feel glad to be sitting there with them. I love them, I really do. But lately, it's just... it's like there's nothing in me that can respond. I just can't seem to feel that happiness right now. \n\nIs that... does that make sense for what you're asking? I really hope that doesn't make me sound like a terrible father.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that your irritability has been happening almost every day, or does it come and go?\n\nPatient: Um... I'm actually not entirely sure. I haven't really been keeping a tally, to be honest. \n\nI mean, I really, really hope it's not every single day. I'd hate to think I'm making things difficult for my team or my family on a daily basis, you know? I try my absolute best to keep it under wraps and just push through it, so... maybe it just comes and goes? I guess I just haven't noticed a strict pattern to it. \n\nI'm sorry, I wish I could give you a clearer answer on that. Is it okay that I'm not totally sure how often it's happening?\n\nDoctor: Can you recall if the days when you feel irritable or emotionally flat are happening more often than not, like more than four or five days out of the week?\n\nPatient: I... gosh, I really hate to think it's that often. Four or five days sounds like a lot, doesn't it? I mean, I definitely try to have good days, especially for my family's sake. \n\nI guess... well, if I'm being completely honest, maybe it is happening more days than not lately. It's just hard to admit that because I really want to be doing better than that. I don't want to be a burden or, you know, that grumpy guy everyone has to walk on eggshells around. \n\nBut yeah, I suppose if I look at the whole week, it's probably more often than not. I'm really trying to work on it, though. I hope that's the kind of answer you were looking for? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right information so we can fix this.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed a decrease in your ability to feel pleasure or a lack of interest in activities that usually bring you joy, even when you're not feeling irritable?\n\nPatient: Um, no, I don't think so. I really try to keep up with my regular stuff. I don't want to just, you know, give up and sit around the house doing nothing. I just want to make sure I'm giving you the clearest picture possible.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed yourself slowing down, feeling less motivated, or having trouble getting things done, even the things you usually handle without a problem?\n\nPatient: Um, no, I wouldn't say I've slowed down. I mean, as a manager, I really can't afford to, right? I have to keep the team moving. So I still get up, get to work, handle the operations... I make sure everything gets done. \n\nI really pride myself on being reliable, so I push through whatever I'm feeling to make sure I'm not dropping the ball. I don't want anyone thinking I'm slacking off or that I can't handle my responsibilities. \n\nI might be a little more tired at the end of the day, but I'm definitely still getting everything done. Does that sound okay? I just want to reassure you that I'm still functioning and taking care of my business.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. I... well, I really try to be present, you know? I want to be a good husband and father. I don't want them to feel like I'm ignoring them or anything. 

But... I guess, sometimes, lately... it's just hard. Like, my wife will make a nice dinner, or the kids will be talking about their soccer games, just like normal, happy stuff. And I know I should feel glad to be there, sitting with them, just enjoying the family time. But... it's like there's just nothing in me that can respond to it. I feel kind of blank inside, even though I'm smiling and nodding along. 

I feel terrible admitting that, I really do. I don't want you to think I don't love my family, because I do, more than anything. It's just this... this block, I guess? I hope that doesn't make me sound like a bad person. I'm really trying to fix it so I can feel those good things with them again. Does that make sense?

Um, well, I guess... I haven't really had any days like that. I mean, I really wish I could say yes, that there are days where I feel totally normal and connected. I want to give you a positive answer on that. 

But, if I'm being completely honest—and I know I need to be honest with you—even on the days where I'm not feeling so on edge or irritable, that... that distance is still there. Like, I might be having a decent day at work, getting things done, no major stress, but when I get home or try to just hang out with friends, I still feel that... that blankness. 

I really try to shake it off, I promise I do. I don't want to be distant. I want to be the guy who laughs and jokes around like I used to. But it's just... it's like a wall I can't quite get over right now, even on a "good" day. 

Is that okay to admit? I just... I don't want it to sound like I'm not trying to get better. I really am. I just want to make sure I'm answering your question the right way.

People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Um, yeah, I guess I'd have to say it's... it's most days right now. I really hate admitting that. I want to tell you it's just a couple of bad days a week, because I know I should be able to shake this off better than I am. I try really hard to put on a good face, especially for my family.

Well, um... I think they've mostly just stayed about the same. I mean, I really hope they haven't gotten worse. I've been trying so hard to keep a lid on it, you know?

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Um, yes, I... I really think they are directly related. I mean, this has all been going on for about three weeks now, exactly since the day of the valve failure and... and the injury at the facility. 

I really wish I could say it was just a coincidence, or that I was just having a stressful month at work, because I hate making excuses for my behavior. But to be completely honest, before that day, I wasn't snapping at people or pulling away from my wife. I was completely fine. 

It's just a little embarrassing to admit that it's been three whole weeks and I haven't been able to just shake it off and get back to normal yet. I know I should be handling it better, especially being a manager. I'm supposed to be the one keeping everyone else calm. I hope I'm not coming across as weak or anything by admitting that it's still affecting me this much. 

Does that... does that answer your question okay? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right timeline so you know exactly what we're dealing with.

And I know I should feel glad to be there, sitting with them, just enjoying the family time. But... it's like there's just nothing in me that can respond to it. I feel kind of blank inside, even though I'm smiling and nodding along. \n\nI feel terrible admitting that, I really do. I don't want you to think I don't love my family, because I do, more than anything. It's just this... this block, I guess? I hope that doesn't make me sound like a bad person. I'm really trying to fix it so I can feel those good things with them again. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Can you recall any days when you haven’t felt irritable—maybe even days when you felt emotionally stable or somewhat good—where you still felt connected and emotionally present with others, or did you still feel that emotional distance even on those better days?\n\nPatient: Um, well, I guess... I haven't really had any days like that. I mean, I really wish I could say yes, that there are days where I feel totally normal and connected. I want to give you a positive answer on that. \n\nBut, if I'm being completely honest—and I know I need to be honest with you—even on the days where I'm not feeling so on edge or irritable, that... that distance is still there. Like, I might be having a decent day at work, getting things done, no major stress, but when I get home or try to just hang out with friends, I still feel that... that blankness. \n\nI really try to shake it off, I promise I do. I don't want to be distant. I want to be the guy who laughs and jokes around like I used to. But it's just... it's like a wall I can't quite get over right now, even on a \"good\" day. \n\nIs that okay to admit? I just... I don't want it to sound like I'm not trying to get better. I really am. I just want to make sure I'm answering your question the right way.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way—this emotional distance and irritability? Has it been going on for months, or has it been more like a year or longer?\n\nPatient: Um, no, it hasn't been that long at all. Not months or a year, definitely not. I wouldn't let it go on that long without trying to get a handle on it, you know? \n\nIt's really just been... about three weeks. It all started right after this incident at work. There was a... a valve failure at the facility, and someone got injured. It was a really intense situation, and I had to manage the fallout. Ever since then, I've just felt kind of off. \n\nSo it's only been a few weeks. I mean, three weeks isn't that long in the grand scheme of things, right? I'm sure it's just a temporary thing from the stress of that day. I just wanted to come in early and get some advice so I can nip it in the bud and get back to being myself. Does that timeline make sense for what I'm describing? I just want to make sure I'm giving you all the right details.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that your irritability and emotional distance tend to occur specifically in response to stressful situations, like work pressures or reminders of the recent incident, rather than happening on their own?\n\nPatient: Well... I really wish I could say it only happens when I'm stressed out about work or thinking about that day. I mean, that would make sense, right? It would be easier to manage if it was just triggered by certain things. I try so hard to keep it together when I'm just at home or doing normal stuff.\n\nBut, if I'm being completely honest—and I know you need the whole picture—it kind of just... happens. Even when things are perfectly calm. Like, I could just be sitting in the living room on a Sunday morning, no work emails, no pressure, and I still feel that... that distance. And the irritability, it just flares up out of nowhere, even when nobody's doing anything wrong. \n\nI feel awful saying that, because it makes it sound like I'm just a grumpy guy for no reason, and I promise I'm really not usually like this. I don't want you to think I'm just lashing out at my family for the fun of it. It's just... it seems to be there all the time right now, no matter what's going on around me. Is that... is that normal for something like this? I hope I'm explaining it okay.\n\nDoctor: Would you say your irritability and emotional distance are occurring on most days, and do they seem to happen regardless of what’s going on around you?\n\nPatient: Um, yeah, I guess I'd have to say it's... it's most days right now. I really hate admitting that. I want to tell you it's just a couple of bad days a week, because I know I should be able to shake this off better than I am. I try really hard to put on a good face, especially for my family. \n\nBut yeah, it does seem to just be there, regardless of what's happening. Like I said, even on a quiet weekend, I'll just feel that wall between me and everyone else, or I'll feel this... this tightness, this irritability, just sitting there in the background. I feel terrible about it, because my wife and kids haven't done anything to deserve me being so closed off. \n\nI really am trying to push past it, I promise. I don't want to be this difficult to be around. Does that... does that answer what you're asking? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right information so you can help me fix this.\n\nDoctor: Have your feelings of irritability and emotional distance remained about the same since they started, or have they gotten worse over time, even without any new stressful events happening?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I think they've mostly just stayed about the same. I mean, I really hope they haven't gotten worse. I've been trying so hard to keep a lid on it, you know? \n\nI don't think it's spiraling or anything like that. It's just sort of... been a steady hum since that day at the plant. I really try to make sure I'm not letting it escalate, especially since there haven't been any new emergencies or anything to justify me getting more upset. \n\nSo, yeah, I'd say it's just been pretty consistent. I'm doing my best to hold the line until I can get this sorted out. Is it a good sign that it hasn't gotten worse? I'm hoping that means it's just a temporary bump in the road.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any other changes in how you feel, like trouble sleeping, changes in your appetite, or feeling down or empty, even outside of the irritability and emotional distance you've described?\n\nPatient: Well, um... my appetite is fine, really. I make sure to eat dinner every night so my wife doesn't worry, and I don't really feel empty or anything like that. I'm trying hard to stay positive for everyone. But... I guess I am having a little trouble with the sleeping part. I really wish I could say I was sleeping like a log, because I know getting a good night's rest is exactly what I'm supposed to be doing to get back on track. \n\nBut, to be completely honest with you... most nights I just lie there in the dark, and it's like I can still hear that alarm going off in my head. I try so hard to block it out and just relax. And then when I finally do manage to doze off, I... well, I wake up sweating. I keep having these dreams about the smoke and hearing people yelling. I try really hard to stay quiet so I don't wake up my wife, but by the time morning comes around, I feel like I never slept at all. \n\nI hate complaining about it, because I know it's probably just my brain trying to process things, and I feel like I should be strong enough to just shut it off and go to sleep like a normal person. Is that... is it normal to have dreams like that after an incident? I just want to make sure I'm answering your questions the right way so we can get this fixed.\n\nDoctor: Would you say your irritability and emotional distance started clearly in response to the valve failure incident at work, and do you think they are directly related to that experience?\n\nPatient: Um, yes, I... I really think they are directly related. I mean, this has all been going on for about three weeks now, exactly since the day of the valve failure and... and the injury at the facility. \n\nI really wish I could say it was just a coincidence, or that I was just having a stressful month at work, because I hate making excuses for my behavior. But to be completely honest, before that day, I wasn't snapping at people or pulling away from my wife. I was completely fine. \n\nIt's just a little embarrassing to admit that it's been three whole weeks and I haven't been able to just shake it off and get back to normal yet. I know I should be handling it better, especially being a manager. I'm supposed to be the one keeping everyone else calm. I hope I'm not coming across as weak or anything by admitting that it's still affecting me this much. \n\nDoes that... does that answer your question okay? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right timeline so you know exactly what we're dealing with."
